# Rendering migration handoff

Updated: 2026-09-13. Latest private wolf high-crack acceptance: **r734 original full paired manifest passes**, all five poses with exact geometry, accepted uploads, exclusive ownership and unchanged pixels. Normal armor remains gated. Latest moving armor two-phase proof: **r707/r709 original full private manifests pass**, five paired stepped poses with per-face lighting, exact geometry, native ownership, unchanged pixel/clock limits and complete process-memory receipts. Normal armor remains gated pending remaining materials and lifecycle proof. Base/stopped armor controls: r690/r691/r692. Normal shield animation: r628; completed static shield grid: r670/r672/r675/r676/r679/r680/r683/r684. Normal special-item scopes: r661/r663/r664/r665/r666/r667/r668; preserve their documented limits. Full-scope completion ledger: `docs/VANILLA-RUST-VULKAN-COMPLETION.md`. Latest entity-preview acceptance: **adult unmarked brown horse in r835**. The r836-r839 remaining base coats and r840-r842 remaining marking layers are implemented and test-validated but await serial paired GPU acceptance because display `:0` currently cannot create a GLX context. Adult Horse, the complete seven-coat baby unmarked saddle grid, the complete baby white-coat marking-and-saddle grid, the first non-white coat-and-marking cross-product, baby diamond-, copper-, iron-, gold-, netherite-, and dyed-leather-armored white Horses, ordinary unchested and chested adult and baby Donkey and Mule, adult Camel and Pig, and warm adult Strider fixtures now have exact body-plus-equipment source gates in both capture trees and the shared verifier; all remain privately admitted and pending paired runtime proof. The standing Warden now has strict temporal Current/Frozen evidence for its body, always-on bioluminescence, both phase-opposed pulsating spots, tendrils and heartbeat. The unaffected Copper Golem has strict body-and-emissive-eyes fixture, route, composition and producer evidence using the same explicit translucent-emissive Rust material. The south-facing Copper Golem Statue now has a separate registered block-entity source gate spanning all four poses, all four oxidation textures, and waxed versus unwaxed registered blocks with exact scoped Rust execution evidence. The adult plains farmer Villager now has strict four-model Current/Frozen fixture, route, composition and producer evidence for its body, biome type, profession and diamond-level layers. The adult plains farmer Zombie Villager now has the equivalent strict four-model source gate for its body, biome type, profession and diamond-level layers. The visible-pumpkin Snow Golem now has an exact body-plus-carved-pumpkin source gate and attached block geometry selects its block-display ownership route. Seventeen item-frame source gates are now implemented: ordinary and glow visible and invisible empty frames, diamond-filled and framed-map content; ordinary and glow diamond and map rotation 3; an ordinary framed map with an exact `minecraft:red_x` decoration; invisible ordinary and glow frames with diamond content; and invisible ordinary and glow framed maps. The content gates correlate exact identities, transforms, vanilla light values and completed Rust execution. The adult red sheep body, tinted undercoat and wool composition has equivalent three-model source gates. Ordinary high-crack Iron Golem overlay, adult tamed Wolf and Cat collars, size-two Slime outer shell, Drowned outer skin, Stray clothing and unshorn Bogged moss compositions also remain pending real paired proof. Powered-creeper, powered-wither and invulnerable-wither fixtures cover both EnergySwirl compositions and the distinct invulnerable base texture with strict Current/Frozen producer evidence; the energy family remains privately gated pending real paired proof. Powered Dragon and Piglin heads now add strict live-animation source gates to the stable skull inventory. Stable north-facing Wither Skeleton, Zombie, and Creeper wall heads now have equivalent exact-type gates. Powered north-facing Dragon and Piglin wall heads now cover the combined wall-orientation and live-animation branch. Standing and north-facing Player heads now bind a fixed resolved offline profile to its exact bundled default skin. The attached ceiling-middle oak hanging sign now covers its distinct vertical-chain model branch. The north-facing wall oak hanging sign now covers the plank-and-normal-chain wall branch. A standing oak sign with authored yellow glowing back text now covers the full-bright outlined text branch and proves back-face submission. Stage-five destruction now has deterministic source gates for the standing-sign atlas-backed Model path, inactive-Conduit ModelPart path and standing Skeleton Skull direct-texture Model path. Next unused capture is **r877**. Newest entries below supersede historical live/next-run entries.

This handoff does not declare whole-game completion. The previously private shield route now follows normal Rust Vulkan ownership. r628 passed the original full manifest without either legacy shield flag; r629 passed the subsequent static/reload control. Historical private manifests remain unchanged.

## Read this first

- **The bounded pre-Iris/DH performance milestone now has controlled measurements and a first architectural optimization.** The harness explicitly selects `dev` or `release`, passes that choice to Gradle, and records the exact Gradle command, native-library path, size, SHA-256, and Rust profile in every artifact; this removes the prior possibility that an up-to-date copy silently reused the last profile built. Two alternating same-save, same-camera 180-frame runs measured dev Rust at 28.51–28.56 ms median (35.0–35.1 FPS) and release Rust at 14.52–14.64 ms (68.3–68.9 FPS), while Java allocation stayed near 13.10 MB/frame. Release therefore cuts median frame time by about 49% without changing the workload. Benchmark counts and byte totals now use dedicated counter samples instead of being mislabeled and scaled as nanosecond phases. The GUI path now reuses immutable model-identity snapshots, avoids defensive-array clones inside the synchronous bridge encoder, and keeps a bounded 256-entry cache of nonanimated item topology keyed by content identity and GUI scale. Animated models and all pose/placement state remain frame-local, and resource reload explicitly invalidates the cache, preserving the separation needed for future Iris passes and DH assets. Two post-cache runs measured 12.73–13.17 ms median (75.9–78.5 FPS), about 11% less median frame time than the release baseline; Java allocation fell about 30% to 9.17 MB/frame and GUI item enqueue fell about 29% to 2.15–2.18 ms. A final instrumented run recorded 900/900 stable-item cache hits during its measured window, 13.27 ms median (75.4 FPS), 2.65 ms median GPU time, and the exact release SHA `b28211914edcd8934072dc42fd79e7c128b73d773bb21a48e4b5bb20a432931b`. A separate standard-validation run completed without crashes, device loss, audit messages, or Vulkan VUIDs. The next optimization target is CPU-side GUI/native submission and packed reusable staging; GPU synchronization and memory-safety checks are not the limiting cost. Evidence: `logs/graphics-audit/perf-profile-ab-20260913/`, `logs/graphics-audit/perf-gui-topology-20260913/`, `logs/graphics-audit/perf-gui-topology-final-20260913/`, and `logs/graphics-audit/perf-gui-topology-validation-20260913/`.

- **Multiple Mauser Kar98 inventory entries no longer exhaust the whole-frame GUI mesh budget, and TACZ GUI meshes now retain Frozen's material and lighting semantics.** The reported frame failed at 64,368 vertices when the next Kar98 requested 10,728 vertices and 16,092 indices; the 236 KiB Bedrock model contains 449 cubes and emits 2,682 visible quads in eleven bounded batches. Java and Rust now share a 1,048,576-vertex/3,145,728-index per-frame bound, enough for a gun-heavy item-browser page while the existing 128 MiB aggregate payload bound remains authoritative. The GUI adapter now uses `entityCutoutNoCull`, the 0.1 alpha cutoff, and entity-preview lighting, carries each Bedrock polygon's transformed normal instead of assigning one batch-wide normal, and omits collapsed zero-area Bedrock faces that Frozen rasterizes as no-ops but Rust correctly rejects as inconsistent triangles. Current and Frozen TACZ sources use the same GUI/fixed positioning transform; the difference was in the Rust GUI adapter after that transform. Focused Java validation passes 537/537 and the Rust GUI-mesh suite passes 58/58. A real RunDev-equivalent `New World (1)` launch rendered the repaired Kar98 in the inventory and first-person view without a GUI capacity/submission failure; evidence is `/tmp/mattmc-mauser-before-split.png`, `/tmp/mattmc-mauser-seven-slots-fixed.png`, and `/tmp/mattmc-mauser-validation-3.log`.

- **RunDev time changes now refresh terrain lighting with the sky.** The Rust whole-frame shell previously never consumed `LightTexture`'s per-tick dirty flag, so the sky read the new world time while terrain continued sampling the cached 16x16 nighttime lightmap. The shell now updates that semantic lightmap before its first world seed. Live validation also exposed that the Fabulous material path staged a Rust-owned lightmap replacement without confirming or discarding its transaction; it now confirms after successful submission and discards a pending replacement on failure. A focused Rust regression replaces generation 1 with generation 2 through two real Fabulous submissions, and the whole-frame ordering contract passes with 449/449 Java production tests. A RunDev-equivalent launch of `New World (1)` at 1920x1012 used the inventory's Night button (`time 18000`) and Day button (`time 6000`): both sky and terrain darkened at night, both immediately brightened at day, and the client exited cleanly without a submission or capacity failure. Evidence: `/tmp/mattmc-night-after-button.png`, `/tmp/mattmc-day-after-button.png`, and `/tmp/mattmc-daylight-validation-2.log`.

- **The RunDev inventory crash is fixed and reproduced as a GUI batch-capacity mismatch in the built-in JEI panel.** The expanded diagnostic caught the exact failure at 1,024 mesh batches but only 4,360/65,536 vertices and 6,540/196,608 indices while opening the ordinary inventory at 1920x1012. MattMC keeps the survival-style `InventoryScreen` in Creative mode and renders its JEI-style browser across the remaining screen width; hundreds of visible block items each preserve independently textured baked faces, so a valid low-payload frame can exceed the former 1,024-batch cap. Java aggregation, Rust FFI decoding, the mesh frontend, and the GUI frontend now share a 16,384-batch bound aligned with the aggregate quad geometry capacity. A real RunDev-equivalent launch of the exact `New World (1)` save at render distance 10 and 1920x1012, with E injected immediately after the player joined, rendered the complete inventory and dense browser while terrain streamed and remained stable past the old failure point. The related fixes remain: VoxelMap no longer creates a full raw-image-cache upload loop (about 97% less FFI image traffic in the comparable diagnostic), the graphics-pass splitter retains only current bindings and reserves `EndPass` space, GUI model capture supports Sodium's optimized writer, whole-frame GUI ownership is retired after presentation, identical immutable image payloads are deduplicated, and capacity failures retain detailed projected-count/request diagnostics. Focused validation passes 542/542 Java tests and 66/66 Rust GUI-mesh tests.

- **The adult creamy diamond-armored Horse begins the non-white coat-by-armor source grid; paired acceptance remains pending.** `horse-creamy-diamond-equipped` requires a visible tamed adult with `Variant.CREAMY`, `Markings.NONE`, actual diamond body armor, and a real saddle. Current selects `horse_creamy.png` and requires correlated creamy body, `horse_body/diamond.png` armor, and saddle meshes plus both completed Rust compositions. Frozen passively records those same three ordinary OpenGL emissions from one entity and position. The integrated structural document rewrites both the inherited base mesh and route decision to the creamy coat, preventing a white-base false positive. Both Java trees compile, production contracts pass 436/436, and the complete graphics harness passes 1044/1044. Real paired pixels, exclusive runtime ownership, the remaining coat-by-armor combinations, resource variants, and bounded lifetime proof remain pending while display `:0` rejects GLX context creation.

- **All 27 registered vanilla block-entity renderer types now have an explicit source ownership inventory; runtime acceptance remains incomplete.** The registrations collapse to 23 render-state families because signs/hanging signs, ordinary/trial spawners, and the three chest types share state and renderer contracts. Twenty-two families enter the common block-entity semantic dispatcher; piston uses its separate moving-block semantic traversal. The inventory covers model and model-part meshes, sign text, beacon and portal procedural materials, structure/test debug geometry, nested spawner entities, and Campfire/Brushable/Shelf/Vault indexed-item producers. The ABI contract now names every registered type and fails when a registration is added without updating the ownership audit. Selected Vulkan suppresses Iris block-entity tracking, requires a registered block-state identity, balances semantic and item scopes, and fails closed at unsupported producer callbacks. This is a source ownership inventory, not family-wide acceptance: representative real fixtures, paired Frozen OpenGL pixels, completed Rust presentation, resource-pack variants, destruction overlays, nested entity/item variants, and bounded reload/retirement proof remain outstanding.

## Goal and scope

The user's ultimate goal is complete rendering through semantic callsites → Rust-implemented explicit VulkanicGAL → Rust Vulkan, including vanilla, Distant Horizons, Iris shader packs, resource packs, and their interactions. There must be no Java Vulkan rendering, hidden fallback, borrowed Iris/DH GPU runtime state, shared native GPU handles, or second presenter.

The working sequence has been narrowed into phases:

1. **Vanilla Rust Vulkan:** terrain, fluids/translucency, GUI/HUD/text, entities and layers, block entities, items/hands, particles, sky/clouds/weather, world effects, post-processing, resource packs, menus/loading, and every remaining vanilla callsite. DH and Iris shader execution are outside this phase except isolation.
2. Distant Horizons through the same Rust-owned frame.
3. Rust-owned Iris shader-pack execution, without DH.
4. Correct combined Iris + DH operation.

`docs/RUST-MIGRATION-PROMPTS.md` contains the later Goal 2–4 prompts. Phase separation does not remove DH/Iris from the ultimate objective and does not mean vanilla is complete.

Completion requires actual supported gameplay, deterministic paired Frozen OpenGL parity, clean validation, bounded resources, and proof of semantic ownership/isolation—not merely compilation, source checks, synthetic GPU scenes, or a favorable screenshot.

## Non-negotiable architecture and workflow

- Callsites supply semantic data/commands. Transitional Java extraction may supply bounded immutable CPU model/resource data; it must not become a renderer/policy engine or copy Java-selected animation frames to simulate Rust ownership.
- VulkanicGAL exposes explicit backend-neutral resources, pipelines, passes, usages, synchronization, submission, completion, and presentation. Rust Vulkan consumes that explicit state directly.
- Only the Rust OpenGL backend may privately reconstruct OpenGL's implicit state machine. Do not solve Vulkan gaps by borrowing GL/Iris/DH runtime state.
- Keep unfinished capabilities unavailable/private, with no fallback. A private diagnostic passing is not normal capability admission.
- Prefer complete narrow vertical slices. Internal defects and missing prerequisites are implementation work, not excuses to stop at an audit.
- Frozen Java OpenGL is authoritative. Never use Frozen/current Java Vulkan for correctness, acceptance, or normal testing.
- Equivalent fixtures, worlds, cameras, settings, packs, timing, and capture provenance are mandatory. Do not loosen thresholds, fit reference pixels to the candidate, freeze clocks, or hide capture defects to get green results.
- **Stop for the user's decision before treating a new suspected Frozen behavior as a bug or changing its rendering behavior.** Permission for a previous specific fix is not blanket permission.
- Watch host memory and disk. There is **no arbitrary 4 GiB limit**: the user wants memory roughly comparable to Frozen and low enough not to crash the workstation. Avoid concurrent builds/test suites and game clients.
- Generated captures/audits belong under the ignored `logs/graphics-audit/`, never loose in the repository root. Do not recreate hundreds of gigabytes of disposable fixtures.

## What has actually improved

### Base equipment view layering integration (ABI57; capture r685 unused)

Added shared native view_layering semantics: projection flags4/8, exclusive validation, and Rust postmultiplication of the view by Frozen's perspective scale4095/4096 or orthographic translateZ1/512. item_foil retains its projection alias and now delegates to that shared calculation. Native MeshResourceKey.view_layering and packed view headers resolve both base flags and existing foil modes; missing/mixed metadata rejects. Model matrices, geometry, projection/fog/shadow header bytes and raster depth policies remain unchanged. Java modelMeshRenderSemantics identifies armor cutout/translucent/decal material declarations, copies only the projection convention and propagates flags through both Model and ModelPart enqueue paths. The same diagnostic property gates those unverified base/trim families, in addition to the existing glint guard.

ABI57 extends the shared instance flags, with no record layout growth (mesh struct ID69 still28fields). Java, native frontend and FFI reject combined projections, mixtures with outline/camera-sorted flags, foil+base-layer flags, nonentity/placed-terrain and block-entity misuse. Ordinary foil keeps flags0 and derives view layering from its explicit kind. Selected source entity/hand preparation explicitly rejects layered instances until it can preserve that metadata, rather than silently consuming it; Iris execution remains outside Goal1. Direct vanilla batch lowering handles the new flags. The separate source path is not claimed implemented.

### Private armor texture/transport/batch integration (ABI56; capture r685 unused)

Current Java's standalone armor submit now has an explicit private gate mattmc.dev.rustArmorFoil, checks copied model/state/identity, selects ENCHANTED_GLINT_ARMOR, and copies the current projection convention as a scalar semantic kind. No Java animation/layering matrix is computed. Existing trident/shield calls retain the item texture and ENTITY foil. A shared extractModelPartMesh overload accepts the selected foil resource and retains original UVs, explicit copied sampling, content-derived mesh identity and existing transactional resource ownership. enqueueCopiedModelFoil accepts the copied foil record while retaining existing queue bounds/checkpoint rollback.

ABI56 adds shared standard-foil modes3(perspective equipment) and4(orthographic equipment) without a new record family or layout growth. Rust kind ArmorOrthographic shares the0.16 UV basis; armor_projection distinguishes layering. MeshResourceKey includes that projection so armor cannot coalesce with ordinary foil or another projection. packed_mesh_uniforms_for_batches writes the Rust-computed layered view followed by unchanged projection/fog/shadow header bytes, rejects missing/mixed/lost layering metadata and retains the existing standard48-byte foil instance stream. The GLINT pipeline still uses Equal depth, no depth writes, and existing Frozen blend semantics. Native and Java GUI-item validation reject both equipment kinds. Public Java armor admission remains closed unless the diagnostic property is set; no normal armor capability claimed.

### Broader work and limits of this handoff

The worktree contains extensive Java/Rust/harness changes beyond shields: GUI/item extraction and raster contracts, item/foil resources, atlas animation, terrain/particle semantics, entity/world rendering, and deterministic parity instrumentation. Earlier work also addressed user-reported Rust OpenGL GUI/text/loading/hand issues and Frozen terrain issues; user acknowledgments are historical feedback, not a current whole-game regression certification.

Retained family diaries include `gui-item-animation-progress.md`, `gui-item-transform-progress.md`, `gui-item-foil-progress.md`, `standard-foil-default-progress.md`, `special-item-foil-progress.md`, `item-translucent-cutout-progress.md`, `gui-atlas-whole-transaction-progress.md`, and `creeper-resource-graph-progress.md`, under `logs/graphics-audit/`. Consult their final accepted evidence and the current admission code when expanding the inventory.

Initial source restriction inventory: `logs/graphics-audit/vanilla-readiness-inventory.md` (2026-09-10). It identifies concrete special-foil and model-foil restrictions; it does not certify the other families.

**No fresh exhaustive family-by-family readiness audit was performed for this document.** Do not turn the presence of code/tests or those diary filenames into a claim that an entire family is complete. The following recent milestones have much more specific evidence.

### Static shields and shared Rust-owned atlas

- Earlier static milestones covered plain shields, patterned shields, enchanted/foil variants, and reload. The diary records normal plain admission at r577, patterned reload at r580, patterned enchanted reload at r583, and plain enchanted control at r584.
- Subsequent work fixed opaque GUI alpha/depth semantics (r590/r591) and solid-model semantic declarations (r592/r593).
- r597/r598 established shared Rust-owned SHIELD atlas source/lifecycle/reload prerequisites for GUI and held consumers. Consumers use one native atlas incarnation, not independent standalone copies of a selected frame.
- Java declares immutable full-sheet animation metadata and semantic ticks/use; Rust owns playback, interpolation, retained pixels, and native resources.
- SHIELD texture ID is signed Java `-34138350`, unsigned Rust `4260828946` (`0xfdf71712`). Nonzero signed Java IDs are valid bit patterns; do not require positivity.

### User-approved Frozen fix: animated shield classification

The discovered baseline defect was specific: non-foil GUI shield icons could retain their first cached raster while the resource-pack texture and held shield animated. The user explicitly authorized fixing Frozen.

Both repositories now have:

- `SpecialModelRenderer.isAnimated(argument)`, default false.
- `ShieldSpecialRenderer.isAnimated`, examining actually selected plain/patterned base and visible pattern materials, respecting the 16-layer limit.
- `SpecialModelWrapper` propagating animation independently of foil.
- `ShieldGuiAnimationTest` regressions for selected/static/animated/reloaded materials, ignoring an unused plain material on colored shields, wrapper/foil behavior, and the visible-layer cutoff.

This did not alter animation clocks, lighting, or selected GPU frames. Frozen's full suite and the paired r600 run verified the fix. No subsequent Frozen renderer changes are implied or authorized by it.

### Explicit native GUI item cache and lifetime fixes

- Java supplies bounded semantic item identities (`GuiItemSemanticIdentities`, maximum 64 retained identities, monotonic IDs not reused by clear) and immutable model identity snapshots.
- `GuiItemCacheRecord(identity, isAnimated)` crosses the explicit bridge; ABI layout 97 has 40 GUI mesh fields, with identity/mode at fields 38/39. Validation rejects invalid identities and unsupported cache combinations.
- Rust cache keys include generation, semantic identity, and raster extent. Composite keys distinguish item identities. Animated items rerasterize; static items reuse their native raster with explicit attachment/read usages. Reload and identity changes retire native targets.
- Discarded preparation no longer publishes an attachment layout as though it had submitted. Layout evidence comes from the target's own accepted pass use or earlier use within the same command transaction.
- A prepare/drop/retry regression found missing vertex/index uploads on retry. Transaction-scoped geometry cache keys now preserve same-transaction deduplication without treating discarded preparation as uploaded geometry.
- An actual Vulkan pixel test found a predicted first-raster submission ID could be consumed by an internal upload before the real raster. The fix **removed `raster_submission`/`mark_rasterized` prediction**: static cache proof is the private target pass's actual accepted submission, queried through `render_pass_last_submission`.
- That same test exposed a three-resource leak during partial image replacement. Teardown now releases dependent mesh descriptors before shared image views/samplers/textures, matching full teardown dependency order.

At r606 the suspected stream defect was reproduced: completing internal asset uploads allowed a second GUI preparation to reuse the same buffer and offset while the first draw remained pending. The failing log is `mesh-retirement-regression-r606.log`.

GUI stream ranges now own Rust `SubmissionUsage` receipts. Prepared command copies retain each receipt; GAL records the actual accepted submission only after backend submit succeeds. Discarding commands releases pending reservations without inventing a submission. Reclamation requires no pending command copies and completed accepted use; bounded backpressure waits only for an eligible range. The old GUI predicted submission parameter is removed. GAL consumes `TrackSubmission` before backend lowering, and canonical ABI serialization excludes these private CPU receipts.

Regressions cover interleaved internal uploads, pending preparations, cancellation/retry, accepted-versus-completed use, bounded backpressure, failed submissions, and retained command copies across multiple submissions. `vulkan_pending_mesh_preparations_match_separate_submissions` additionally compares actual Vulkan pixels for two preparations combined into one submission against separately submitted controls and verifies created==destroyed resources. These prove the tested GUI stream lifetime cases, not universal lifetime correctness for all renderers.

## Normal shield animation admission and historical diagnostics

`AtlasAnimationResource.shieldLifecycleEnabled()` follows `RustGalVulkanWholeFrameMode.enabled()`. TextureAtlas, held/model extraction and GUI collection share that normal lifecycle. The legacy `mattmc.dev.rustGalShieldAtlas` and `mattmc.dev.rustGalShieldAtlasAnimation` properties no longer authorize production rendering; capture provenance rejects their presence for normal admission.

`model_item_foil_parity_report` now requires strict phase/source/pixel evidence plus `shield_animation_normal_admission`: a normal unflagged receipt, exact Current Rust/Frozen OpenGL modes, healthy captures, clean Vulkan validation, and the exact correlated Rust-only presentation receipt with no Java Vulkan or LOD route and the same acquired/presented image. A phase diagnostic alone remains unadmitted. Unknown oracle combinations still reject; thresholds and Frozen-derived reference pixels are unchanged.

r628 (`logs/graphics-audit/shield-normal-animation-admission-r628`) has original manifest success and capability admission true, all 17 probes exact and all four material source checks passing. Both screenshots were inspected. Current peak RSS was 4,839,108 KiB; Frozen RSS was unavailable, so this is not paired memory proof. r629 (`shield-normal-static-control-r629`) has original manifest success, all seven probes exact and both regions passing; both screenshots were inspected. Neither run used private shield flags.

Validation: corrected full Java suite 2,909 passed / 2 skipped (2,911 total), using the existing ByteBuddy experimental compatibility switch for Java 25 in the test JVM only. An architecture test rejected an extra presentation-state query; that query was removed, keeping ownership selection in the mode class. The combined Python run had 576 passes and one wording assertion failure; the reason was corrected, then all 26 reference/pack tests passed (50 subtests). All 551 harness tests passed in the combined run. No Rust source changed for admission; the last full native result remains r621, 1,724 passed / 2 ignored, including bounded atlas replacement, retry and GUI view retirement tests.

The following records describe historical private development and do not override current admission:

The plain fixture is a resource-only three-frame shield pack: red, green, blue sheets; playback `[(2,3), (0,5), (2,2), (1,7)]`, total 17 ticks. It exercises nonzero initial sheet, repeated sheet, unequal durations, and optional interpolation.

The `shield-pattern-animation` / `shield-pattern-animation-interpolated` resource-only fixtures animate `entity/shield/cross.png`, preserving its vanilla alpha mask and shading while changing source colors. Their metadata uses the same 17-tick sequence. The shared harness selects `mattmc.dev.graphicsAuditShieldAnimationSprite=entity/shield/cross` in BOTH launchers. A bounded capture-only observation selector was added to Current and Frozen; it reads existing playback and does not change renderer behavior, clocks, or uploads. Frozen's suite passed after that diagnostic-only change.

New Frozen-derived oracles distinguish plain foil and patterned variants. Authored components, selected sprite, source/mip hashes, native ownership, retained phase, and presentation correlation remain mandatory; no tolerance was relaxed. Unknown variant/phase combinations have no oracle and fail the diagnostic. At those historical checkpoints the main gate still returned false. Original r607–r609 manifests remain failed because admission is closed; their `shield-animation-variant-supplement.json` records the later validated supporting evidence without rewriting those manifests.

At r610–r612, `shield_animation_scenarios.py` became the shared material/scene mapping for pack generation, BOTH launcher observation properties, source identity checks, and material-specific pixel oracles. New scenarios `shield-body-animation[-interpolated]`, `shield-dye-animation[-interpolated]`, and `shield-border-animation[-interpolated]` name `entity/shield_base`, `entity/shield/base`, and `entity/shield/border` respectively. Each changes only that original texture's colors/animation metadata while preserving its alpha mask and shading. Existing plain/cross scenes retain their inputs. No Java, Rust, or Frozen source changed in this latest turn.

Matching evidence uses the **retained submitted source pixels**, not just the latest clock. An empty-use tick may advance the clock while the last visible pixels remain correct. Native source traces explicitly are not GPU readbacks; final paired screenshots provide separate output evidence. Presentation correlation, source/mip hashes, owner/sprite identity, and Frozen-derived probes are all necessary.

## Commands for the next agent

These are instructions for future work, **not commands run for this handoff**. Use unique ignored log names and check memory/disk/processes first. Run GPU/native tests serially; driver-global EGL/Vulkan/OpenAL state matters. Poll the existing process rather than restarting a still-running test.

```bash
# Full current suite; no game clients running concurrently.
CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 ./gradlew --no-daemon test > logs/graphics-audit/<unique>-tests.log 2>&1

# Targeted native regression (replace TEST_FILTER).
CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/home/matt/Documents/Repos/MattMC/build/rust/target ALSOFT_DRIVERS=null cargo test --manifest-path src/main/rust/Cargo.toml --locked TEST_FILTER -- --test-threads=1

python3 -m unittest discover -s DevUtils/Audit -p 'test_shield_animation*.py'
python3 -m unittest discover -s DevUtils/Audit -p 'test_graphics_harness.py'
```

Paired normal animated shield capture template; replace `UNIQUE_RUN` and select the intended phase/scenario. Do not overwrite previous evidence:

```bash
CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 MATTMC_ATLAS_TRACE_TEXTURE=4260828946 MATTMC_ATLAS_TRACE_SPRITE=1 \
python3 DevUtils/Common/graphics_harness.py capture --profile extended \
  --mode current-rust-vulkan-shaders-off --mode frozen-opengl-shaders-off \
  --frozen-repo /home/matt/Documents/Repos/MattMC_JavaPerfTesting/MattMC \
  --world Origin --world-static-terrain-scenario real-world \
  --hotbar-item-fixture shield --selected-hotbar-slot 1 \
  --gui-resource-pack-scenario shield-animation-interpolated --world-resource-reload \
  --jvm-arg=-Dmattmc.dev.graphicsAuditShieldAnimation=true \
  --jvm-arg=-Dmattmc.dev.graphicsAuditGuiItemCapturePhase=0 \
  --jvm-arg=-Dmattmc.dev.graphicsAuditSliceMetrics=true \
  --jvm-arg=-Dmattmc.dev.deterministicCameraCapture.frozenInternalScreenshots=true \
  --rust-full-gameplay-attachments --validation standard \
  --artifact-root logs/graphics-audit/UNIQUE_RUN --artifact-dir logs/graphics-audit/UNIQUE_RUN \
  --artifact-preserve-current-run > logs/graphics-audit/UNIQUE_RUN-driver.log 2>&1
```

For ordinary static control omit the animation pack/phase/trace diagnostics, retaining reload and Frozen internal screenshots as appropriate to the cache path being tested.

Inspect `graphics_audit_manifest.json`, each mode's `capture/run-01/graphics_audit_artifact.json`, `paired_visual_static_terrain/visual_parity_report.json`, deterministic capture receipts, and `capture_request_01_initial.ack.json`. Read selected JSON fields: metrics can be enormous. Actual client logs are under `capture/run-01/capture/runClient_*.log`. Use `rg --files --hidden --no-ignore` to find ignored evidence.

## Artifact retention and workstation safety

At the last checkpoint disk had approximately 397 GiB available; idle host memory approximately 24 GiB available out of 31 GiB. These are historical observations, not current limits. Monitor available RAM, swap pressure, process RSS, and disk during new runs. Do not use a 4 GiB cap or claim memory parity without paired measurements.

Generated per-run canonical fixture copies through r631 were removed **only after verified recovery**. Images, reports, logs, recovery overlays, and the immutable base were retained. Each disposable run copy was about 2.6 GiB.

Immutable recovery base (do not delete):

```text
logs/graphics-audit/dh-isolated-moon-r100/.canonical-fixtures/Origin-migration-gate-real-world-vanilla-hidden-hidden-hidden-hidden-hidden-auto-dh-none-mattmc-cross-repo-fixture-v2/run
```

After both clients and driver terminate, use `DevUtils/Common/canonical_recovery.py --base BASE --source EXACT_GENERATED_RUN --output ARTIFACT/recovery`, retain its log, and verify terminal success plus a receipt matching the exact source. Only then remove that explicitly validated generated `run` directory. Never delete the immutable base or entire evidence tree. Report what was removed and whether recovery exists.
